The reason why I am starting this journal is to be honest with myself and not to take a trade which has not been planned in advance. This should help me in controlling my itchy finger trades....trying to find a trading opportunity where most likely there is none. I would be posting my charts and analysis for people to read and scrtunize and share their opinions regarding the same. Cheers! Edit: 26042013 Summary of my trading style: HTF Demand/Supply - Engulfing on H1 or higher - Find the DP/origin of the move on LTFs - Pending order Edit: 200713 I will save you some time, if you want to learn about my way of trading, start reading from PAGE 4. DAILY: On the daily chart, price has been in an uptrend since July 2012, currently standing at a place from where it was rejected twice earlier, April 2012 and September 2012, Currently also, price is starting to project weakness, having been rejected for the third time in December. Image 1: Daily linegraph for trendline and S/R flip. Black thick line is the S/R flip. Image 2: Daily chart showing rejections of the price from the supply zone. The supply zone marked in green happens to be the Weekly and Daily supply zone. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary 4/49 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Image 3: H4 chart. The take home message for me from this chart is break of the uptrend by the engulf of the source of the final upmove shown in light blue rectangle. The white areas in the green zone are the untouched decisions made by the price to move lower. Image4: M30 chart shows the decision made by the price to break the S/R line. Decision is marked in Golden color. The price is now compressing into the decision. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary 5/49 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Image5: M5 chart helps us to zoom into the area where it was decided price is too expensive to go above the S/R flip and price moved further down. On M5 you can see the source of the engulfing, once the price went above that line, I was watching the price on m1, holding my finger on the sell button, watching the PA on TICK CHART, as long as the ticks continued to move higher, I did not press the sell button. Sorry I can't edit and upload a chart at the moment, hopefully my questions makes sense to you Thanks, Rex Hi Rex, Thanks for asking the question. Here is the answer: If you see just below my entry, there is a blue rectangle, only partially visible. It says H1 FTR. The chart which you are looking at is an M5. You have a valid reasoning for going short on m5 when price comes back after making the engulf, but the problem is there is no HTF supply above it to support the engulf on the short https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=225 29/42 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 10 - RTM Community side. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HTF tells you where the price is standing currently with respect to big players and LTF gives you entries. Try not to fixate on LTFs only coz you want to take more trades. Instead, go out to H4, daily, weekly, monthly and mark out each and every demand and supply which is created. wait for the price to come back to these zones and then watch the PA on LTFs. Then when you have the signal, trade it. By LTFs I dont mean M1,M5 but atleast H1 to get engulf, then pin point your entries on M5, M1 to enter the trade. In short: "demand/supply H4 or higher, watch PA on return to these zones on M30/H1, got engulf, pin point your entries on M1/5/15. SL above the zone. Target, oppostite zone on the time frame on which you are taking the trade" Good luck and keep the questions coming. Can you please make some editing on the chart and then put your question again? Thanks. Hey Sunny, Below is the 4hr chart and the 1hr chart. Notice that if you would wait for price to enter the 4hr zone and engulf on the 4hr zone, you would be taking the entry to the far right. But if you wait for price to enter the 4hr zone and then go down to an hourly time frame, you would enter much earlier. In some cases the hourly would give you a few opportunities to enter. If you watched the hourly you would have tried the short to the far left. Then you would have possibly shorted for the much farther move if you got back in on the trade marked with the blue arrows. If you don't take second trades you might have even missed that trade all together. So my question is do you enter the trades based on the engulfs from the 4hr engulf (or whatever time frame you use).....or do you watch lower time frames as they unfold. So with the 4hr you would wait for price to enter the 4hr zone, engulf downward on the 4hr and then look at lower time frames for your entries at pockets of supply. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=250 17/41 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Thanks, H Attachments: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=250 18/41 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 11 - RTM Community This shall be the beginning of an end to a very long journey for me. We need to identify which engulfs are most significant and trade from them. In that chart most likely on the HTF there was a supply area above the high, price did not want to go any higher and engulfed the last significant demand which lead to a push up to the supply, this shows buyers are no longer in control and we should be looking to short which will be from the origin of the engulfing of the demand. For price to return to the origin it needs to go higher and so will be engulfing on the move up, we know these won't be significant as all price is trying to do is each the origin to get in more sellers and push price down. That's how I view it anyway but I would like to see Sunny's response as well. The following user(s) said Thank You: Chanya, sunnytrader1984, windorz 28/41 QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=250 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 11 - RTM Community THANK YOU EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! #267 sunnytrader1984 Offline Associate Trader Posts: 375 Thank you received: 732 Hi Chanya, Hi Rex Thanks Rex for answering Chanya's question. Core to looking for engulfing trading is where it happens. HTF supply/demand, SR flips coming from daily, weekly or monthly TFs. These are the areas where engulfings become significant, otherwise like Rex said, they happen everywhere. If I would say one thing, it would be, try to figure out who is stronger : Bulls or Bears. once you can answer this question, trade setup will become logical. Try to look where are the profit margins, bullish or bearish. Good luck guys and have a nice weekend. If I would say something, it would be, plan your setup and then trade your setup. Put your SL in place and TP in place. One of them will get hit. If you planned your setup well and you trust your setup, occasional losses will happen but if you good R:R in place, you will come out positive from it. Answer me one thing, sitting in front of screen and watching price move once you are in the trade, does it help the price move better in your direction? If the answer is yes, then sit and watch but if the answer is no, leave PO and forget it. Check it once the candle is closed, depending if you are trading from H4 or Daily. Hi Romant, If you want me to tell you in one line why I took all those trades, the answer would be: The only common thing in all my trades is ENGULFING. Now, you have to understand the engul ng, looks good, looks weak etc, Where it forms, ofcourse if you see it is forming in HTF Demand and Supply zones, it is good. If not then what, can be ftr, can support/resistance, can be Moving averages. If it is happening, there must be a reason, and the reason is, big money is interested in pushing the price in the direction of engulf. That is all the con rmation I need. When you see any of my charts, they are most likely marked with my reason to enter a trade. I really try to make my charts look as simple as possible they can be, coz I myself dont understand as much as some of these other guys do. I try to keep it simple so that it can make some sense in my stupid head. If you think, I dont get loosers, I do, but they are small. Sometimes I am saved from falling into the trap of https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=275 19/42 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 12 - RTM Community a bad trade because there is no engulf. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT If I was to give you one thing that can help you become successful, I would give you ENGULFING. Take it, use it, understand it, why it works, why it did not work. Take trades based on engul ng.....they will start coming together. I hope my rant makes some kind of sense. Keep posting your charts, setups and you will get comments on them, if not from me, then somebody else on this community. Have a nice day. Excellent sir!. Scenario: I took this trade, both in demo as well as in my live account. With a well sought and thought out analysis, chose my targets and stoploss. When I got into the trade I knew I was going to take it to the take profit, no shortcuts but I also knew there was 'DBD supply sitting right in between at 76, which can stop my trade. So, the greed took the best of me and I closed my trade, for a decent 50 pips, but the point of story is "I sold my trade cheaply to the bastards" Here is something we all must understand, when we are planning our trades, we must know where our stop losses should be and where our most logical take profits should be. There must be a reason for every why and why not, if you dont have a reason, there should be no trade. I really believe here at RTM, we have the education good enuf for all of us to be able to trade profitably with people like IF, Doctor and many others around on this website, who are more than willing to help us become good at it but at the end of the day, what we need is, to believe in ourselves. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! #184 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=175 11/36 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT sunnytrader1984 Offline Associate Trader Posts: 375 Thank you received: 732 Hi Hoto, There is no single rule to find where you want to trade. We trade supply and demand, lot of people trade ranges, support and resistance, trend line, Fibonaccis and what not. All these places are good enough to see price action. A DBD or a RBD, they both are a good places to look for PA, and if PA is happening at any place where it makes sense to happen, our duty is to take that trade. We dont need to make a million analysis before taking every trade....keep your Reward:Risk high enough, trade with some kind of logical setup which gives you winning trades, and you are on your way. Settle down on a setup which you like to trade, observe it like hell, in every time frame, every situation, every place it forms, trade it and see when it works, when it does not work. Think why it worked and why it did not work. You can ask 1000 questions, no body here at RTM will mind, we all are here to help each other, that is the motive or RTM, but in the end, it will come down to you and your trading style. Understand your trading style like the back of your hand and you will be all good. You dont need a 100 ways to trade, find 1 that suits your style. Feel free to ask as many questions as you want, I will try my best to answer them. If I can not, I am sure somebody else will definitely. Good luck man. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=175 12/36 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 8 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: DrSwing, Danish, Enlightenme, cuimingzhao HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT There are no lost opportunities in market, only lost accounts! Napoleon Hill QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! #187 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=175 16/36 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Pedini Offline Master Trader Posts: 568 Thank you received: 2620 Hey hoto, As Sunny says, keep the questions coming. To wait for engulfs on the higher TF only will take quite a long time and we wouldn't get many trades. We can do this successfully on lower TF as well. Also, I would absolutely look at a combination of time frames when price is in a daily or H4 zone, even 5min charts. The important thing to remember here is we are looking for reactions, for signs price wants to move away from an important level. These important levels can be, as sunny says anything, they can be flip zones, SD zones, SR levels. Once price reaches these levels in any time-frame we can look for engulfings on a smaller time frame to trade them. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! #191 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=175 24/36 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Alientrader Offline Banned Posts: 432 Thank you received: 370 Hello Hotochin, I have marked the origin of the engulf I am speaking of. It is certainly a nice level and when price comes back it will definitely be a level to consider carefully. I think you are speaking of this origin being a reaction to a demand level left which it does appear it was. But I am speaking of this most current engulf north. I understand an engulf to be an important overcoming of a significant portion of price action such that the probability that price will continue in that direction is higher. In this case price engulfed north ( I have the point of the engulf marked by the white line). Institutional money causes such an engulf and when it does it leaves it's signature which is the creation of more levels. In this case when price engulfed north pockets of demand were created. The daily chart doesn't show them that well but the H4 chart is more revealing. Before price reached the supply marked overhead a return to one of the demand levels marked had an increased probability of causing price to rise to the supply level which it has and is currently in. Now that price is in a significant supply zone dynamics could change with sellers stepping in and driving price back down again to one of the lower https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=175 25/36 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 8 - RTM Community demand zones. I don't know what will happen but when big money steps in they will HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT again leave their signatures by engulfing south and creating new supply levels. I am not opposed to trading levels midstream if the dynamics are in favor. Alientrader So in continuation of this idea, you consider the midzones for targets mostly, not for opening positions and the EXTREME zone at the bottom will be THE ONLY one where you'll be looking for longs? Since I am trading H4 and up I will open positions at clearly defined "midzone" levels. I have found that price responds quite nicely to midzone levels and often may not revisit the origin for some time. By the time it does revisit the origin the dynamics may have changed and price may violate the origin. If you are trading M1 and M5 it's probably better to go with extremes. I'll see if I can find some charts supporting this. Hi Romant, If you want me to tell you in one line why I took all those trades, the answer would be: The only common thing in all my trades is ENGULFING. Now, you have to understand the engulfing, looks good, looks weak etc, Where it forms, ofcourse if you see it is forming in HTF Demand and Supply zones, it is good. If not then what, can be ftr, can support/resistance, can be Moving averages. If it is happening, there must be a reason, and the reason is, big money is interested in pushing the price in the direction of engulf. That is all the confirmation I need. When you see any of my charts, they are most likely marked with my reason to enter a trade. I really try to make my charts look as simple as possible they can be, coz I myself dont understand as much as some of these other guys do. I try to keep it simple so that it can make some sense in my stupid head. If you think, I dont get loosers, I do, but they are small. Will bear that in mind Could I just trouble you with a few more questions? 1) Why did you draw your daily supply like that and not with the upper edge at the top of the cap? Ans. This is just the way I like to draw my zones, I only like to use 2 candles to draw the levels. the candle which moved away from a certain point and the previous candle, that is it. 2) In general how do you manage your trades? I think I can understand your entries enough, and if I'm not wrong your target is the first unengulfed demand/supply. However is there any time where you'll tighten/trail your stop? I noticed that there are occasions where you could already be largely in profit, price then retraces a fair bit to wipe out a lot of that profit and sometimes even put you back in drawdown. How do you deal with that? I personally would have freaked out. :P Is it accurate to say that you usually don't interfere (tighten stops https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=200 25/35 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 9 - RTM Community etc) with your trades until they hit either your TP or SL? Do you even move your HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT stop to breakeven once price has moved quite a distance away? I'm asking because there is a school of thought that says never let a winning trade turn into a losing one (meaning move ur stop to BE once in substantial profits), but somehow that seems to be counterproductive with the strategies used in RTM. Here are some examples of your previous trades to illustrate what I mean. Ans. I dont pay attention to single candle engulfings but I pay more attention to swing engulfings. If you understand the reason why I enter the trade then use it in reverse, and you will have my reason for exiting a trade. When the price approaches to a significant demand or supply level belonging to higher time frames, I follow the price on m30, and when the last swing high/low gets engulfed, I exit. This is my way of trailing. Now you can figure out why I did not close the trade on first of my charts. On the gold trade, when price makes engulf of a level, it is more often than not going to the next level above or below, so I knew what I was targeting before I opened the trade. About price coming back in, it doesnt scare me because when I look up, it made a new engulfing, which clearly said to me, we are going up, if you want you can add another position at the point when price came down again https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1569-sunnytrader1984-s-trading-diary?start=200 26/35 7/27/2018 Sunnytrader1984's trading DIARY! - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Hope this helps.
